G.9
and noting that
\textup{tr}[\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}(t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]=1
.

The set
\big\{U^{(l,k)}_{\textup{M}_{0,k}\textup{P}_{k}\textup{M}_{0,k-1}\textup{P}_{k-1}}\big\}_{k=1}^{N_{g}-1}
uniquely determine the full set
\big\{U^{(l,k)}_{\textup{M}_{0,k}\textup{P}_{k}\textup{M}_{0,k-1}\textup{P}_{k-1}}\big\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
since

\displaystyle U^{(l,k)}_{\textup{M}_{0,k}\textup{P}_{k}\textup{M}_{0,k-1}\textup{P}_{k-1}}=U^{(l,k+qN_{g})}_{\textup{M}_{0,k+qN_{g}}\textup{P}_{k+qN_{g}}\textup{M}_{0,k-1+qN_{g}}\textup{P}_{k-1+qN_{g}}}

(G.14)

for
q\in{\mathbbm{N}_{>0}}
.

Finally, we now introduce the idealised states which we wish to proximate by the actual dynamics generated by the dynamical semigroup. We denote them by
\big\{\ket{[t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{[t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{C}}\big\}_{k=0}^{\infty}
and define them as follows

\displaystyle\ket{[t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}

\displaystyle=U^{(l,k+1)}_{\textup{M}_{0,k+1}\textup{P}_{k+1}\textup{M}_{0,k}\textup{P}_{k}}U(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\ket{[t_{k-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}},

(G.15)

\displaystyle\ket{[\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}

\displaystyle=\ket{\rho(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}=\ket{\mathbb{m}_{l,1}}_{\textup{M}_{0,1}\textup{P}_{1}}\ket{(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\bar{\textup{M}}_{0,1}\textup{W}\textup{L}\bar{\textup{P}}_{1}}\ket{0}_{\textup{C}}

(G.16)

\displaystyle\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}

\displaystyle=\delta(k)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}t_{k}}{2T_{0}}}\ket{t_{k}}_{\textup{C}},\qquad\delta(k)=\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}\,\beta(k)N_{g}},\qquad\gamma_{0}=\bar{\gamma}_{0}\,d^{\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}},

(G.17)

\displaystyle\ket{[t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{C}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}}{\|\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}}=\ket{t_{k}}_{\textup{C}}=\ket{\Psi(t_{k}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}},

(G.18)

k=1,2,3\ldots
where
\bar{\gamma}_{0}>0
is any positive
d
-independent parameter,
\beta{(k)}
is the largest non-negative integer such that

\displaystyle k=\beta(k)\,N_{g}+r,

(G.19)

for some
0\leq r<N_{g}
. Notice that the choice
\ket{t_{k}}_{\textup{C}}=\ket{\Psi(t_{k}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}}
is identical to that of the proofs to
Theorems

2
and
3
(namely
eq.

C.34
). Recall that
\ket{\Psi(t_{k}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}}
is periodic:
\ket{\Psi(t_{k}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}}=\ket{\Psi(t_{k+N_{g}}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}}
, c.f.
eq.

E.34
. And where
U(\mathbb{m}_{l,N_{g}}):={\mathbbm{1}}
(since there is no gate being applied in this time step), and we assume periodic boundary conditions
U(\mathbb{m}_{l,k+qN_{g}})=U(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})
,
k=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}
,
q\in{\mathbbm{N}_{>0}}
. This condition is only relevant in the event that the renewal process occurs late, which as 1) in
Theorem

4
shows, is extremely unlikely.
19
19

19

We could have alternatively imposed a hard “cut-off” condition where we define the unitary to be the identity for
k\geq N_{g}
and a similar theorem would follow.

The only constraint on the unitaries
\big\{U^{(l,k)}_{\textup{M}_{0,k}\textup{P}_{k}\textup{M}_{0,k-1}\textup{P}_{k-1}}\big\}_{k=2}^{N_{g}-1}
is that they are such that
eq.

V.3
is satisfied. This amounts to property

\displaystyle\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}=\ket{\mathbb{m}_{l,k+1}}_{\textup{M}_{0,{k+1}}\textup{P}_{k+1}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\bar{\textup{M}}_{0,k+1}\textup{L}\textup{W}\bar{\textup{P}}_{k+1}},

(G.20)

for
k=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}-2
(for
k=0
, the state
\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{k}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}
already satisfies condition
eq.

G.20
by virtue of
eq.

G.16
).
Notice that due to the periodicity of the control states,
\{\ket{\Psi(t_{k}d/T_{0})}_{\textup{C}}\}_{k}
, there are implied periodic boundary conditions on the states
\{\ket{\mathbb{m}_{l,k+1}}_{\textup{M}_{0,{k+1}}\textup{P}_{k+1}}\}_{k}
in
eq.

G.20
. Physically, the nature of the imposed boundary conditions is not very relevant since the probability of the dynamics not being renewed after each cycle of the control is very small as 1) in
Theorem

4
shows.

We now make a definition needed for the following lemma. For
j\in{\mathbbm{N}_{>0}}
, let
\beta^{\prime}(j)
be the largest non-negative integer such that

\displaystyle j=\beta^{\prime}(j)N_{g}+r,

(G.21)

for some
r\in\{1,2,\ldots,N_{g}-1\}
. (Note that this differs from the definition of
\beta(k)
in
eq.

G.19
, due to a difference in the domain or
r
.)

G.2
Proof of
Theorem

4

Before proving
Theorem

4
, we start by proving a lemma which we will use multiple times in the proof of
Theorem

4
.

Lemma G.1
.

For all
l,j\in{\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 0}}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}>0

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\big\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.22)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}(1+\beta^{\prime}(j))}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}}}g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}(E_{0})\,E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}},

(G.23)

where the r.h.s. is
\varepsilon_{b}
-independent. Where as previously,
g(\bar{\varepsilon})>0
is
E_{0}
-independent while
\textup{poly}(E_{0})
is an
\bar{\varepsilon}
-independent polynomial in
E_{0}
. The product
g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}(E_{0})
is independent of
l
,
j
and
\varepsilon_{b}
.

Recall that
E_{0}:=\textup{tr}[H_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{C}}\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{C}}^{0}]
[as per definition
III.6
]. In the present context, the relevant mean energy is
\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]
. However, writing

\displaystyle\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]=E_{0}+\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}

(G.24)

one can show that the difference
\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}
is superpolynomially small, namely
\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}={\textup{SupPolyDecay}_{\displaystyle{\,\bar{\varepsilon}}}\,}(E_{0})
. This is because of two points: 1) both Hamiltonians only differ by their interaction terms and both states are close to orthogonal to these. 2) the reduced states on
C
are the same. It is not essential as a stand-alone result, so we only prove it when strictly needed at the end of the proof of
Theorem

in
eq.

G.86
.

Ultimately, in
Theorem

4
we wish to describe our results as a function of power
P^{\text{in}}
. As we will show in the poof of
Theorem

4
below,
P^{\text{in}}
is also proportional to
E_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof will follow similar steps to that of
Theorem

2
, but with important differences. We will only briefly cover the steps which follow analogously to that of
Theorem

2
for brevity.

To start with, note that we can write
\ket{\rho(t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
in the form

\displaystyle\ \ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}=\Delta_{j}\Delta_{j-1}\ldots\Delta_{1}\,\ket{\rho(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}},\qquad\Delta_{j}=U^{(l,j+1)}_{\textup{M}_{0,j+1}\textup{P}_{j+1}\textup{M}_{0,j}\textup{P}_{j}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}

(G.25)

for
j=0,1,2,\ldots
. Therefore, by making the association
\{\ket{\Phi_{j}}=\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
, applying
Lemma

C.4
we find for
j=0,1,2,\ldots
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\,\big\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.26)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\,\sum_{q=1}^{j}\big\|\ket{[t_{q}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{q}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}-U^{(l,q+1)}_{\textup{M}_{0,q+1}\textup{P}_{q+1}\textup{M}_{0,q}\textup{P}_{q}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.27)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{j}\big\|\ket{[t_{q}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q)\ket{t_{q}}_{\textup{C}}-U^{(l,q+1)}_{\textup{M}_{0,q+1}\textup{P}_{q+1}\textup{M}_{0,q}\textup{P}_{q}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta({q-1})\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.28)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{j}\big\|U(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q)\ket{t_{q}}_{\textup{C}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q-1)\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2},

(G.29)

where in the third line we have defined
G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}:=G_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}-\mathrm{i}{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}}
while in the last line we used the unitary invariance of the two-norm.

Define

\displaystyle G^{{(k)\prime}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}:=\begin{cases}H_{\textup{C}}+I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}}^{(k)}\otimes I_{\textup{C}}^{(k)}&\mbox{ if }k=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}-1\\
H_{\textup{C}}-\mathrm{i}\gamma_{0}I_{\textup{C}}^{(N_{g})}&\mbox{ if }k=N_{g}\end{cases}

(G.30)

Thus adding and subtracting an appropriate term in
eq.

G.29
we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\,\big\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.31)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{j}\big\|U(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q)\ket{t_{q}}_{\textup{C}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{(q)\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q-1)\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.32)

\displaystyle\;\;\quad+\delta(q-1)\,\big\|\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{(q)\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}.

(G.33)

To proceed, for line
G.32
we can replace

with

\displaystyle G^{(q,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}_{\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}:=\begin{cases}H_{\textup{C}}+I_{\textup{L}}^{(q,\,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}\otimes I_{\textup{C}}^{(q)}&\mbox{ if }q=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}-1\text{ and }\mathbb{m}_{l,q}\in\mathcal{G}\\

H_{\textup{C}}-\mathrm{i}\,\gamma_{0}I_{\textup{C}}^{(N_{g})}&\mbox{ if }q=N_{g}\end{cases}

since

is proportional to

due to

G.20
.

C.10
to the end of the proof. Crucially, we note that the final results still follow even when the generator is not trace-preserving nor self-adjoint (such as is the case for

). Thus we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\,\big\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.35)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=1}^{j}\bigg(\big\|\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q)\ket{t_{q}}_{\textup{C}}-U^{\dagger}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{(q,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}_{\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q-1)\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.36)

\displaystyle+\delta(q-1)\,t_{1}\max_{x\in[0,t_{1}]}\big\|\left(G^{(q)\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}-G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\,G^{(q)\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q-1)\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}\bigg)

(G.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{j}\bigg(\big\|\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q)\ket{t_{q}}_{\textup{C}}-U^{\dagger}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}t_{1}G^{(q,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}_{\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\delta(q-1)\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}

(G.38)

\displaystyle+\delta(q-1)\,t_{1}\max_{x\in[0,t_{1}]}\big\|\left(G^{(q,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}_{\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}-G^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\,G^{(q,\mathbb{m}_{l,q})}_{\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{[t_{q-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{t_{q-1}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}\bigg),

where in the last equality we have noted that we can exchange

for the same reasons as this exchange was possible before. Recall

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}I_{\textup{W}}^{(k,\,\mathbb{m}_{l,k})}}&\mbox{ if }\mathbb{m}_{l,k}={\bf 0}\text{ and }k\neq N_{g}\\
{\mathbbm{1}}&\mbox{ if }k=N_{g}.\end{cases}

(G.40)

Therefore, by expanding

\textup{M}_{0}\textup{P}
) we can bound line

analogously to as in the proof of

As for upper bounding line

(G.41)

(G.42)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}(q-1)\left(\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{0}}+3\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}+\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}_{d}(d)}\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{0}\int_{k-d/N_{g}}^{k}dyI_{\textup{C},d}^{(N_{g})}(y)}|\psi_{\textup{nor}}(d;k)|^{2}\right)

(G.43)

(G.44)

(G.46)

\displaystyle\quad+A_{\textup{nor}}^{2}\bigg(2\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi}{8}\frac{d^{2}}{\sigma^{2}N_{g}^{2}}}+d\max_{q\in[-1,1]}\,\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{0}\frac{2\pi}{d}\int_{-1/2+q/4}^{1/2-q/4}dy^{\prime\prime}\bar{V}_{0}\left(\frac{2\pi}{N_{g}}y^{\prime\prime}+x_{0}^{(N_{g})}\right)}\bigg)\Bigg)

(G.48)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}(q-1)\left(\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{0}}+3\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}+\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}^{2}+A_{\textup{nor}}^{2}\left(2\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi}{8}\frac{d^{2}}{\sigma^{2}N_{g}^{2}}}+d\,\mathrm{e}^{-2\gamma_{0}\mathstrut\vskip 8.5359pt}\right)\right),

(G.50)

where in line

we have employed Theorem IX.1 (

) from

]
now with the pure imaginary potential function. In line

. We have then performed a change of variable for

. In line

and maximised over them individually. Over the

and last interval, we have upper bounded the exponentiated integral by one, while we have bounded the modulus-squared wave function by one in the middle interval. Finally, we have performed a change of variable for

We now calculate
\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}
noting that it takes on a slightly different expression to that of previous proofs due to the relevant potential function having the

prefactor. Our potential function

satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in{\mathbbm{N}_{>0}}}\left(2\max_{x\in[0,2\pi]}\left|\frac{d^{(k-1)}}{dx^{(k-1)}}\bar{V}_{0}(x)\right|\right)^{1/k}\leq 2nC_{0},

and

was any upper bound to the l.h.s. Before, we thus set

[See Eqs. (F217) and (F218) pg 63 in and text between them in

Furthermore, independently of how

\displaystyle d^{\epsilon_{5}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{\bar{v}\sigma},\qquad\epsilon_{5}>0,

(G.52)

\displaystyle\bar{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\alpha_{0}\kappa}{\ln\left(\pi\alpha_{0}\sigma^{2}\right)}b,

(G.53)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=d^{\epsilon_{6}},

(G.54)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{5}

\displaystyle<\epsilon_{6},

(G.55)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\nu}(t,d)

\displaystyle=|t|\frac{d}{T_{0}}\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma^{3}}{\bar{v}\sigma^{2}/d+1}\right)^{1/2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{d^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\pi}{4}\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{\left(\frac{d}{\sigma^{2}}+\bar{v}\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(|t|\frac{d^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1\right)\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi}{4}\frac{d^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma^{2}}\right),

(G.56)

where in our notation

]
). These give rise to

.

We now have that the potential function of interest is
\gamma_{0}\bar{V}_{0}(\cdot)
, and hence the value for
\varepsilon_{\nu}(t,d)
will be modified. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in{\mathbbm{N}_{>0}}}\left(2\max_{x\in[0,2\pi]}\left|\frac{d^{(k-1)}}{dx^{(k-1)}}\gamma_{0}\bar{V}_{0}(x)\right|\,\right)^{1/k}\leq 2n\gamma_{0}C_{0},

(G.57)

leading to
b=2n\gamma_{0}C_{0}
.
We recall that
\gamma
was chosen in
eq.

G.17
with the parametrization

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}=\bar{\gamma}_{0}d^{\epsilon_{p}},

(G.58)

with
\epsilon_{p}=\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}
, and where we assume
\bar{\gamma}_{0}
to be
d
-independent. let us now justify this parametrization (i.e. we will now write
\epsilon_{p}
, and show why setting
\epsilon_{p}=\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}
is a reasonable choice). From above we find that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\nu}(t,d)

\displaystyle=|t|\,\textup{poly}(d)\exp{\left(-\frac{\pi}{4}\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{(\bar{\gamma}_{0}+d^{\epsilon_{5}}/\sigma)^{2}}d^{2(\epsilon_{5}-\epsilon_{p})}\right)}.

(G.59)

This reduces to
eq.

C.40
in the limit
\bar{\gamma}_{0}\to 1
,
\epsilon_{p}\to 0
as expected. From
eq.

G.59
we observe that we need to choose
\epsilon_{p}
such that
\epsilon_{5}-\epsilon_{p}>0
and parametrize
\varepsilon
in terms of
\bar{\varepsilon}
. To do so, recall
\epsilon_{5}=\eta\bar{\varepsilon}
(
eq.

C.122
) and that in the case under consideration, where the optimal logical frequency
f
is asymptotically achievable, we have using
eq.

C.154

\displaystyle\epsilon_{5}=\eta\bar{\varepsilon}=\frac{2}{2\bar{\varepsilon}+2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}+1}\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}

(G.60)

where we set
\epsilon_{p}=\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{5}-\epsilon_{p}=\left(\frac{2}{2\bar{\varepsilon}+2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}+1}-1\right)\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}

(G.61)

for
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(1-\sqrt{3}/2,0)
so that the r.h.s. is positive. Therefore, in this parameter range the quantities
\|\ket{\varepsilon(t_{1},d)}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}
in
eq.

G.50
decay exponentially in
d^{2(\epsilon_{5}-\epsilon_{p})}
for
q=N_{g}
(for
q=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}-1
they decay exponentially in
d^{2\epsilon_{5}}
since they are given by
eq.

C.40
). Furthermore, from
eq.

G.58
and
eq.

C.129
we have that the other terms also decay exponentially in
d^{x}
for some
x>0
solely determined by
\bar{\varepsilon}

We thus have that
\big\|\ket{\rho(t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}
is upper bounded by the r.h.s. of
eq.

C.101
up to the replacement of
\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{k})
with
\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})
since the gate being applied is distinct in the case at hand. Putting everything together we thus obtain for
j\in{\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 0}}

\displaystyle\big\|

\displaystyle\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}-\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\big\|_{2}\leq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{j}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\,\delta(k-1)\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}(E_{0})\,E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}

(G.62)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\sum_{k=0}^{\beta^{\prime}(j)}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,kN_{g}+q})\,\delta(kN_{g}+q-1)\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}(E_{0})\,E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}

(G.63)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\beta^{\prime}(j)}\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}k}\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}(E_{0})\,E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}},

(G.64)

where in line
G.64
, we have taken into account the definition of
\delta(k)
in
eq.

G.17
.
∎

Finally we are in a stage to prove
Theorem

4
. Below we state a more explicit version of it, which is what we will prove. In particular, we state more explicitly the form which the functions
{\textup{SupPolyDecay}_{\displaystyle{\,\bar{\varepsilon}}}\,}(\cdot)
take (One can readily check that they belong to said function class). Like before,
g(\bar{\varepsilon})>0
(for
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
) is a
P^{\text{in}}
-independent function of
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
, while
\textup{poly}(P^{\text{in}})
is an
\bar{\varepsilon}
-independent polynomial in
P^{\text{in}}
. They are both
l
-independent.

Theorem
4

(
[
More explicit than main-text version.
]
Nonequilibrium steady-state quantum frequential computers exist)
.

For all gate sets
\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{G}}
, initial gate sequences
(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})_{l,k}
with elements in
\mathcal{G}
, and initial logical states
\ket{0}_{\textup{L}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}_{\textup{L}})
, there exists
\ket{0}_{\textup{C}}
,
\{\ket{t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}}_{\textup{C}}\}_{j=1,2,\ldots,N_{g};l\in{\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 0}}}
,
N_{g}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}
parametrised by the power
P^{\textup{in}}>0
and a dimensionless parameter
\bar{\varepsilon}
(where elements
\ket{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{C}}
, satisfy
eq.

V.5
), such that for all
j=1,2,3,\ldots,N_{g}
;
l\in{\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 0}}
and fixed
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1/6)
, the following large-
P^{\textup{in}}
scaling hold simultaneously

Given that
l\in{\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 0}}
renewals occurred in the time interval
[0,\tau_{l}]
, the probability that the next renewal occurs in the interval
[\tau_{l}+T_{0}-t_{1},\tau_{l}+T_{0}]
is:

\displaystyle\int_{T_{0}-t_{1}}^{T_{0}}\mathrm{d}t\,{P}(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})=1-\varepsilon_{r},\qquad 0<\varepsilon_{r}\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}({P^{\textup{in}}})\,{P^{\textup{in}}}^{-1/(2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}})},

(G.65)

The deviations in the state between renewals are small: For
j=1,2,\ldots,N_{g}
,

\displaystyle T\Big(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}),\,\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}\otimes\rho_{\textup{C}}{[t_{j}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}\Big)\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{j}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}({P^{\textup{in}}})\,{P^{\textup{in}}}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}},

(G.66)

The gate frequency has the asymptomatically optimal scaling in terms of power:

\displaystyle f=\frac{1}{T_{0}}\left({T_{0}^{2}}\,{P^{\textup{in}}}\right)^{1-\bar{\varepsilon}}+\delta f^{\prime},\qquad|\delta f^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{T_{0}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\textup{poly}({P^{\textup{in}}}){P^{\textup{in}}}^{-1/(2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}})}\right)\text{ as }{P^{\textup{in}}}\to\infty.

(G.67)

Proof.

We start with the proof of 1). This requires the calculation of the probability that the
(l+1){{}^{\text{th}}}
renewal occurs in the interval
(\tau_{l}+t_{N_{g}-1},\tau_{l}+t_{N_{g}})
, namely

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{r}=1-\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{t_{N_{g}-1}+\epsilon}^{t_{N_{g}}-\epsilon}\mathrm{d}t\,P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})

(G.68)

The probability,
P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})
, can be evaluate by conditioning on not renewing in the interval
(0,\tau_{l})
given that the
l^{\text{th}}
renewal occurred at time
\tau_{l}
, followed by the
(l+1)^{\text{th}}
renewal occurring at time
t
. This can be calculated by applying the renewal generator
\mathcal{D}_{\textup{C}}^{\textup{re}}(\cdot)
to
\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
followed by taking the trace. Thus from
eqs.

F.3
and
G.11
we find that
P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})=\textup{tr}[2V_{\textup{C}}\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}\!\!\bra{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}]
. For
\epsilon>0
, in the interval
t\in(t_{N_{g}-1},t_{N_{g}-1}+\epsilon)
,
\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(t-t_{N_{g}-1})G_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
and in the interval
t\in(t_{N_{g}}-\epsilon,t_{N_{g}})
,
\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(t-t_{N_{g}-1})G_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}}\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
. Therefore, we can solve the integral analytically to find

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{r}=\,

\displaystyle 1-\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{t_{N_{g}-1}+\epsilon}^{t_{N_{g}}-\epsilon}\mathrm{d}tP(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})

(G.69)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1-\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Bigg(\textup{tr}\big[\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\varepsilon+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}\!\!\bra{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\varepsilon+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}\big]

(G.70)

\displaystyle-\textup{tr}\big[\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}}-\varepsilon+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}\!\!\bra{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}}-\varepsilon+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}\big]\Bigg)

(G.71)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 1+\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}\|^{2}_{2}.

(G.72)

We can now use
Lemma

G.1
to exchange
\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
and
\ket{\rho\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}\textup{P}}
for

\ket{[t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}
and
\ket{[t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}
respectively up to the small errors dictated by the r.h.s. of
eq.

G.23
. Recall that
\|\ket{[t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{N_{g}}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}
is exponentially small in
d^{\bar{\varepsilon}^{2}}
, while
\|\ket{[t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}]}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{P}}\ket{\bm{[\!\![\!\![\!\![}t_{N_{g}-1}+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{]\!\!]\!\!]\!\!]}}_{\textup{C}}\|_{2}^{2}=\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon_{b}}=1-\varepsilon_{b}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{b})^{2}
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{T_{0}-t_{1}}^{T_{0}}\mathrm{d}t\,{P}(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})=1-\varepsilon_{r},\qquad 0<\varepsilon_{r}\leq\varepsilon_{b}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{b})^{2}+\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}({E_{0}})\,{E_{0}}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

(G.73)

We now set the free parameter
\varepsilon_{b}
. We choose

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{b}=\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)E_{0}^{-1/(2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}})}.

(G.74)

[The reason for this choice will become apparent later when bounding
|\delta E_{1}^{\text{no re}}|
. In particular, this choice of scaling will be relevant in line
G.134
].
Thus plugging in to
eq.

G.73
we find

\displaystyle\int_{T_{0}-t_{1}}^{T_{0}}\mathrm{d}t\,{P}(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})=1-\varepsilon_{r},\qquad 0<\varepsilon_{r}\leq\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,k})\right)g(\bar{\varepsilon})\,\textup{poly}({E_{0}})\,{E_{0}}^{-1/(2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}})}.

(G.75)

We now calculate
\braket{E^{\text{re}}}
as a function of
E_{0}
. Since from
eq.

V.6
we have
P^{\text{in}}=\braket{E^{\text{re}}}/T_{0}
, this in conjunction with
eq.

G.75
will allow us to prove item 1) in
Theorem

4
.

\displaystyle\braket{E^{\text{re}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\textup{tr}[H_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}^{\prime}\,\mathcal{D}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}^{\text{re}}(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}))]

(G.76)

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\textup{tr}[H_{\textup{C}}\,\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{\textup{C}}\otimes\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}v_{j}{}_{\textup{C}}\!\bra{\theta_{j}}\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})\ket{\theta_{j}}_{\textup{C}}]

(G.77)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{l=1}^{N_{g}-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\textup{tr}\left[\left(I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}}^{(l)}\otimes I_{\textup{C}}^{(l)}\right)\left(\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{\textup{C}}\otimes\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}v_{j}{}_{\textup{C}}\!\bra{\theta_{j}}\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})\ket{\theta_{j}}_{\textup{C}}\right)\right]

(G.78)

\displaystyle={}_{\textup{C}}\!\braket{0|H_{\textup{C}}|0}_{\textup{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})

(G.79)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{l=1}^{N_{g}-1}\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{C}}^{(l)}\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{\textup{C}}]\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}}^{(l)}\otimes V_{\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]

(G.80)

\displaystyle={}_{\textup{C}}\!\braket{0|H_{\textup{C}}|0}_{\textup{C}}

(G.81)

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=1}^{N_{g}-1}\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{C}}^{(l)}\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{\textup{C}}]\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}}^{(l)}\frac{d}{ds}\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]

(G.82)

\displaystyle={}_{\textup{C}}\!\braket{0|H_{\textup{C}}|0}_{\textup{C}}+\sum_{l=1}^{N_{g}-1}\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{C}}^{(l)}\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{\textup{C}}]\,\textup{tr}[I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}}^{(l)}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]

(G.83)

\displaystyle=\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]

(G.84)

\displaystyle=E_{0}+\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}

(G.85)

Where in
eq.

G.84
, we have used
eq.

G.7
and where

\displaystyle\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})]-\textup{tr}[H_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{C}}^{0}]

(G.86)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}-1}\textup{tr}\big[I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}^{(q)}\ket{\mathbb{m}_{l,1}}\!\!\bra{\mathbb{m}_{l,1}}_{\textup{M}_{0,1}\textup{P}_{1}}\ket{(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}\!\!\bra{(\tau_{l}|\tau_{l})}_{\bar{\textup{M}}_{0,1}\textup{W}\textup{L}\bar{\textup{P}}_{1}}\big]\textup{tr}\big[I_{\textup{C}}^{(q)}\ket{\Psi(0)}\!\!\bra{\Psi(0)}_{\textup{C}}\big]

(G.87)

\displaystyle-\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\textup{tr}\big[I_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{C}}^{(q)}\ket{\mathbb{m}_{l,1}}\!\!\bra{\mathbb{m}_{l,1}}_{\textup{M}_{0,1}}\rho^{0}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}}\big]\textup{tr}\big[I_{\textup{C}}^{(q)}\ket{\Psi(0)}\!\!\bra{\Psi(0)}_{\textup{C}}\big],

(G.88)

\displaystyle|\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

(G.89)

In inequalities
G.89
and
G.88
, we have recalled definition
III.6
and noted that the interaction terms have a vanishing contribution to the energy as proven in the proof of
Theorem

3
from
eq.

C.144
onwards.

Thus

\displaystyle P^{\textup{in}}=\braket{E^{\text{re}}}/T_{0}=E_{0}+\delta E^{\text{re}}_{1}/T_{0}.

(G.90)

Therefore, there exists
x_{0}(\bar{\varepsilon})>0
,
P_{0}(\bar{\varepsilon})>0
such that
P^{\textup{in}}\geq x_{0}(\bar{\varepsilon})E_{0}/T_{0}
for all
E_{0}\geq E_{00}(\bar{\varepsilon})
,
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1/6)
. This concludes the proof of item 1), [namely
eq.

G.65
in
Theorem

4
].

For item 2) [namely
eq.

G.66
in
Theorem

4
], start by noting that
Lemma

G.1
can easily be repeated for the normalised version of the states in
eq.

G.23
. Then recalling that the two-norm distance between two normalised states is an upper bound to the trance distance between said states (recall
Lemma

C.5
), we obtain item 2) up to a replacement of
P^{\textup{in}}
with
E_{0}
and up to a trace over the purifying system
P
. Using
eq.

G.85
, we can then convert from
E_{0}
to
P^{\textup{in}}
, and we can trace out the purification by noting that the trace distance is contractive under partial trace.

We now prove item 3) [namely
eq.

G.67
in
Theorem

4
]. Since we are using the same parametrizations as in
Theorem

2
, for case b), we have that
eq.

C.155
holds, namely

\displaystyle f=\frac{1}{T_{0}}\left(\frac{T_{0}}{2\pi\tilde{n}_{0}}E_{0}\right)^{1-\bar{\varepsilon}}+\delta f^{\prime},\qquad|\delta f^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{T_{0}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\textup{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\right)\text{ as }E_{0}\to\infty.

(G.91)

Recall that
\tilde{n}_{0}
is a free parameter of the model in the interval
(0,1)
[see text below
eq.

C.129
]. We can therefore choose
\tilde{n}_{0}=1/(2\pi\kappa_{1})
. Finally, to convert from
E_{0}
to
P^{\textup{in}}
, we substitute for
E_{0}
using using
eqs.

G.90
and
G.89
followed by Tailor expanding about the point
\delta E_{1}^{\textup{re}}=0
analogously to the expansion in
\delta d
in
eq.

C.152
. This concludes the derivation of item 3)   [namely
eq.

G.67
in
Theorem

4
].
∎

The following lemma demonstrates that, up to vanishing errors, that the amount of dissipated power
P^{\textup{diss}}
, is upper bounded by the consumed power
P^{\textup{in}}
, up to a vanishing error term.

Lemma G.2
.

For the quantum frequential computer of
Theorem

4
, the dissipation is upper bounded by

\displaystyle P^{\textup{diss}}\leq P^{\textup{in}}+\delta P^{(+)}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}(P^{\textup{in}}),

(G.92)

where, for all fixed
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,1/6)
, we have
\delta P^{(+)}_{\bar{\varepsilon}}(P^{\textup{in}})\to 0
as
P^{\textup{in}}\to\infty
.

Proof.

Since
P^{\textup{diss}}:=\braket{E^{\text{no re}}}/T_{0}
, we need to upper bound
\braket{E^{\text{no re}}}
. We will establish said bound in terms of
E_{0}
and then convert this to abound in terms of
P^{\textup{in}}
.

Observe that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}]=\,\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\mathcal{D}^{\textup{no re}}_{\textup{C}}\left(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}\right)],

(G.93)

in the intervals where
\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}]
is differentiable. Therefore, defining
N(t)
as the largest integer
N
s.t.
t_{N+1}<t

Recalling
eq.

V.7
we have

\displaystyle\braket{E^{\text{no re}}}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})\int_{0}^{t}\textup{tr}[{H}^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\mathcal{D}^{\textup{no re}}_{\textup{C}}\left(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}\right)]\mathrm{d}s.

(G.94)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})\times

(G.95)

\displaystyle\Bigg[\left(\sum_{j=0}^{N(t)}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{t_{j}+\epsilon}^{t_{j+1}-\epsilon}\!\!\!\!\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\mathcal{D}^{\textup{no re}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\left(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}\right)]\mathrm{d}s\right)

(G.96)

\displaystyle+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{t_{N+1}+\epsilon}^{t}\!\!\!\!\!\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\mathcal{D}^{\textup{no re}}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\left(\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}s+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}\right)\mathrm{d}s\Bigg]

(G.97)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,P(t+\tau_{l},+1|\tau_{l})

(G.98)

(G.99)

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\bigg(\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}t+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}]-\textup{tr}[H^{\prime}_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\,\rho_{\textup{M}_{0}\textup{L}\textup{W}\textup{C}}\bm{(\!\!\!(}t_{N+1}+\epsilon+\tau_{l}|\tau_{l}\bm{)\!\!\!)}]\bigg)

(G.127)

\displaystyle\quad+g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}}{2T_{0}}t_{j}}\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}(1+\beta^{\prime}(j))}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}}}\right)

(G.128)

\displaystyle\leq g(\bar{\epsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})\,\mathrm{e}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\epsilon}}}\,\frac{N_{g}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-(\gamma_{0}N_{g}+\varepsilon_{b}/2)}}

(G.129)

\displaystyle\quad+g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\left((N_{g}+1)\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\varepsilon_{b}j}{2}}\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}(1+j)}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{0}N_{g}}}\right)

(G.130)

\displaystyle\leq g(\bar{\epsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})\,\mathrm{e}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\epsilon}}}

(G.131)

\displaystyle\quad+g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon_{b}/2}}\right).

\displaystyle=g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{N_{g}}\tilde{d}(\mathbb{m}_{l,q})\right)\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{b}+\mathcal{O}({\varepsilon_{b}})^{2}}\right).

(G.133)

\displaystyle\leq g(\bar{\varepsilon})\text{poly}(E_{0})E_{0}^{-1/(2\sqrt{\bar{\varepsilon}})}

(G.134)

In lines
